The plan keeps data checks and task walks alongside that review.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'import type { PermissionFlag } from "@/hooks/useUserPermissions";\r\n\r\n// The classroom rail as data. Behavior (active state, click handlers) stays in\r\n// ClassroomLayout, keyed by id — this file owns only what the rail contains,\r\n// so the Account layout editor and the rail render from one source of truth.\r\n\r\nexport type RailGroupId = "classroom" | "study" | "review" | "support";\r\n\r\nexport type RailItemId =\r\n  | "home"\r\n  | "notes"\r\n  | "work"\r\n  | "drill"\r\n  | "testing"\r\n  | "bootcamps"\r\n  | "activities"\r\n  | "journal"\r\n  | "flagged"\r\n  | "analytics"\r\n  | "history"\r\n  | "admissions"\r\n  | "messages"\r\n  | "schedule";\r\n\r\nexport interface RailItem {\r\n  id: RailItemId;\r\n  label: string;\r\n  group: RailGroupId;\r\n  /** Empty = available to every signed-in student. */\r\n  flags: PermissionFlag[];\r\n  /**\r\n   * Mirrors the `allowSignedIn` prop on this item\'s route. Where the route lets\r\n   * any signed-in user through regardless of `flags`, the rail must not draw a\r\n   * padlock from those same flags — a padlock over a route anyone can open is\r\n   * a lie the student can disprove by typing the URL.\r\n   *\r\n   * Keep this in step with `src/App.tsx`. `railCatalog.test.ts` pins the pairs.\r\n   */\r\n  allowSignedIn?: boolean;\r\n  /** Section items open the side list and render a › chevron when unlocked. */\r\n  section?: boolean;\r\n  /** Home can never be hidden — it is the guaranteed way back. */\r\n  required?: boolean;\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport const RAIL_GROUPS: { id: RailGroupId; title: string }[] = [\r\n  { id: "classroom", title: "Classroom" },\r\n  { id: "study", title: "Study" },\r\n  { id: "review", title: "Review" },\r\n  { id: "support", title: "Support" },\r\n];\r\n\r\nexport const RAIL_CATALOG: RailItem[] = [\r\n  { id: "home", label: "Home", group: "classroom", flags: ["has_classroom_access"], allowSignedIn: true, required: true },\r\n  { id: "drill", label: "Drill", group: "classroom", flags: ["has_drill_access"] },\r\n  // The Testing Centre is where a student sits something under test conditions:\r\n  // one real timed section, or a full practice test. It is deliberately its own\r\n  // rail entry rather than a tab inside Drill — drilling is practice you stop\r\n  // and think inside, and a sitting is not, so putting them behind one door\r\n  // invites starting a 35:00 clock by accident.\r\n  { id: "testing", label: "Testing Centre", group: "classroom", flags: ["has_drill_access"] },\r\n  { id: "work", label: "Assigned work", group: "classroom", flags: ["has_classroom_access"], section: true },\r\n  { id: "notes", label: "Notes and recaps", group: "study", flags: ["has_classroom_access"], section: true },\r\n  { id: "bootcamps", label: "Bootcamps", group: "study", flags: ["has_bootcamp_access"] },\r\n  { id: "activities", label: "Activities", group: "study", flags: ["has_activity_access"] },\r\n  { id: "journal", label: "Wrong answers", group: "review", flags: ["has_journal_access", "has_waj_access"], section: true },\r\n  { id: "flagged", label: "Flagged", group: "review", flags: ["has_flagged_access"], section: true },\r\n  { id: "analytics", label: "Analytics", group: "review", flags: ["has_analytics_access"] },\r\n  // Reference, not a paid feature: what the schools a student is aiming at\r\n  // actually admitted. Open to anyone signed in, like Home.\r\n  { id: "admissions", label: "Admissions", group: "review", flags: ["has_classroom_access"], allowSignedIn: true },\r\n  { id: "history", label: "History", group: "review", flags: ["has_analytics_access"] },\r\n  { id: "messages", label: "Messages", group: "support", flags: ["has_chat_access"] },\r\n  // Schedule owns booking too: one rail entry, two stage tabs (Schedule | Book\r\n  // a session). Two adjacent Support entries for the same errand opened\r\n  // unrelated-looking screens, and the empty Schedule had no way to book at all.\r\n  { id: "schedule", label: "Schedule", group: "support", flags: ["has_schedule_access"], allowSignedIn: true },\r\n];\r\n\r\nexport interface RailPrefs {\r\n  /** Item ids in display order per group. Missing ids append in catalog order; unknown ids are dropped. */\r\n  order: Partial<Record<RailGroupId, string[]>>;\r\n  /** Item ids the student chose to hide. Required items are never hidden. */\r\n  hidden: string[];\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function defaultRailPrefs(): RailPrefs {\r\n  return { order: {}, hidden: [] };\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function normalizeRailPrefs(raw: unknown): RailPrefs {\r\n  if (!raw || typeof raw !== "object") return defaultRailPrefs();\r\n  const r = raw as Partial<RailPrefs>;\r\n  const validIds = new Set(RAIL_CATALOG.map((i) => i.id));\r\n  const order: RailPrefs["order"] = {};\r\n  if (r.order && typeof r.order === "object") {\r\n    for (const group of RAIL_GROUPS) {\r\n      const list = (r.order as Record<string, unknown>)[group.id];\r\n      if (Array.isArray(list)) {\r\n        order[group.id] = list.filter(\r\n          (id): id is string => typeof id === "string" && validIds.has(id as RailItemId),\r\n        );\r\n      }\r\n    }\r\n  }\r\n  const hidden = Array.isArray(r.hidden)\r\n    ? r.hidden.filter((id): id is string => {\r\n        if (typeof id !== "string" || !validIds.has(id as RailItemId)) return false;\r\n        const item = RAIL_CATALOG.find((i) => i.id === id);\r\n        return !item?.required;\r\n      })\r\n    : [];\r\n  return { order, hidden };\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Resolve prefs into the per-group item lists the rail renders.\r\n * includeHidden keeps hidden items in place (the layout editor needs them).\r\n */\r\nexport function resolveRailGroups(\r\n  prefs: RailPrefs,\r\n  opts?: { includeHidden?: boolean },\r\n): {\r\n  id: RailGroupId;\r\n  title: string;\r\n  items: RailItem[];\r\n}[] {\r\n  const hidden = new Set(opts?.includeHidden ? [] : prefs.hidden);\r\n  return RAIL_GROUPS.map((group) => {\r\n    const catalogItems = RAIL_CATALOG.filter((i) => i.group === group.id);\r\n    const byId = new Map(catalogItems.map((i) => [i.id as string, i]));\r\n    const ordered: RailItem[] = [];\r\n    for (const id of prefs.order[group.id] ?? []) {\r\n      const item = byId.get(id);\r\n      if (item && !ordered.includes(item)) ordered.push(item);\r\n    }\r\n    for (const item of catalogItems) {\r\n      if (!ordered.includes(item)) ordered.push(item);\r\n    }\r\n    return {\r\n      ...group,\r\n      items: ordered.filter((i) => i.required || !hidden.has(i.id)),\r\n    };\r\n  }).filter((group) => group.items.length > 0);\r\n}\r\n'}]

The\r\n// legacy orbital/dock/sidebar keys in the same jsonb are dead (the Foyer they\r\n// customized no longer exists) and are preserved untouched on write.\r\n\r\nexport function useRailPrefs() {\r\n  const { user } = useAuth();\r\n  const qc = useQueryClient();\r\n  const userId = user?.id;\r\n\r\n  const query = useQuery({\r\n    queryKey: ["rail-prefs", userId],\r\n    enabled: !!userId,\r\n    staleTime: 60_000,\r\n    refetchOnWindowFocus: false,\r\n    queryFn: async (): Promise<RailPrefs> => {\r\n      const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n        .from("profiles")\r\n        .select("layout_prefs")\r\n        .eq("class_id", userId!)\r\n        .maybeSingle();\r\n      if (error) throw error;\r\n      const raw = (data as { layout_prefs?: Record<string, unknown> } | null)\r\n        ?.layout_prefs;\r\n      return normalizeRailPrefs(raw?.classroom_rail);\r\n    },\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  const save = useMutation({\r\n    mutationFn: async (next: RailPrefs) => {\r\n      if (!userId) throw new Error("No user");\r\n      // Read-modify-write so the legacy layout_prefs keys survive.\r\n      const { data, error: readError } = await supabase\r\n        .from("profiles")\r\n        .select("layout_prefs")\r\n        .eq("class_id", userId)\r\n        .maybeSingle();\r\n      if (readError) throw readError;\r\n      const existing =\r\n        (data as { layout_prefs?: Record<string, unknown> } | null)\r\n          ?.layout_prefs ?? {};\r\n      const payload = { ...existing, classroom_rail: next };\r\n      const { error } = await (supabase as unknown as {\r\n        from: (t: string) => {\r\n          update: (v: unknown) => {\r\n            eq: (k: string, v: string) => Promise<{ error: Error | null }>;\r\n          };\r\n        };\r\n      })\r\n        .from("profiles")\r\n        .update({ layout_prefs: payload })\r\n        .eq("class_id", userId);\r\n      if (error) throw error;\r\n      return next;\r\n    },\r\n    onMutate: async (next) => {\r\n      await qc.cancelQueries({ queryKey: ["rail-prefs", userId] });\r\n      const prev = qc.getQueryData<RailPrefs>(["rail-prefs", userId]);\r\n      qc.setQueryData(["rail-prefs", userId], next);\r\n      return { prev };\r\n    },\r\n    onError: (_err, _next, ctx) => {\r\n      if (ctx?.prev) qc.setQueryData(["rail-prefs", userId], ctx.prev);\r\n    },\r\n    onSettled: () => {\r\n      qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["rail-prefs", userId] });\r\n    },\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  const prefs = useMemo<RailPrefs>(\r\n    () => query.data ?? defaultRailPrefs(),\r\n    [query.data],\r\n  );\r\n\r\n  return {\r\n    prefs,\r\n    loading: query.isLoading,\r\n    save: save.mutateAsync,\r\n    saving: save.isPending,\r\n  };\r\n}\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# Classroom\r\n\r\nThe student\'s own room: the first surface a 1:1 student lands on. It is a reading surface, not a workspace. Its language is second person, and the coach is never named in the furniture.\r\n\r\n## Terms\r\n\r\n**Classroom frame** — the persistent layout every Classroom route is drawn in: the cream left rail, an optional middle list, and the white reading surface. Implemented by `ClassroomLayout`. Navigating between surfaces it draws swaps the content only and never remounts the rail. A page "sits in the Classroom frame" regardless of its URL; `/flagged` and `/journal` both do.\r\n\r\n**StudentShell** — the older, separate frame with its own 240px rail, still drawing `/mainhub`, `/practice` and the rest. A student moving between a Classroom-frame page and a StudentShell page sees the whole page rebuild. Reducing that boundary is ongoing work, one surface at a time.\r\n\r\n**Reading column** — the white content column. Most routes use one reading column on the right. A Wrong answers detail uses a four-column Classroom frame: cream rail, entries list, vertical question, and review panel. Below its journal-only breakpoints the entries list becomes an overlay and the review panel moves under the question.\r\n\r\n**Drill landing workspace** — the pre-question surface at `/classroom/drill`, drawn inside the Classroom frame. It includes drill selection, launch controls, practice insights, and today\'s work; it excludes the official question-answering experience.\r\n_Avoid_: Drill page (when the question experience could be confused with the landing surface), Mainhub\r\n\r\n**Question finder** — the one control that turns something a student can say about a question into that question opened. It takes free text, matched against question text, and it takes a question address (PT 90, Section 1, Question 12) typed or picked from PrepTest / Section / Question selectors. It searches both banks and groups its results by bank, Logical Reasoning first, because a hit lands differently in each: an LR hit opens that one question, an RC hit opens its whole passage with every question on it. The finder is a finding aid, not the scope of the work it opens. It appears on Classroom Home and in the Drill landing workspace; it never builds a set, it hands that to the Drill console.\r\n_Avoid_: search bar, command palette, spotlight, quick access, find bar\r\n\r\n**Drill console** — the control surface inside the Drill landing workspace where a student assembles a set: the filters, the preview of the exact set, and the start action. Finding one named question is not its job; that is the Question finder.\r\n_Avoid_: Control pane, picker card\r\n\r\n**Drill canvas** — the route-specific visual composition to the right of the persistent Classroom rail, containing the Drill hero, Drill console, practice/insight workspace, and Today focus. The Classroom rail is not part of the canvas.\r\n_Avoid_: Drill side pane, dashboard grid\r\n\r\n**Assigned work** — work addressed to a student\'s account by the coach: homework, guided drills, Activities, bootcamp modules, plan tasks, timed sections. It belongs to the student it is addressed to and appears only on that student\'s surfaces (Classroom Home, the Drill landing workspace\'s Today focus, the Assigned work section).\r\n\r\n**A teacher is never the student** — a row addressed to the coach\'s own account is not assigned work, on any Classroom surface. The coach sees what they assigned through the teacher view of that student (the Hub), never as work of their own. Verification writes and test rows addressed to the admin account are noise, not a to-do.\r\n_Avoid_: my tasks (for the coach), self-assignment (as a testing path)\r\n\r\n**Section** — a middle-column list paired with the reading column: Notes and recaps, Assigned work, Flagged. A section is picked in the rail, lists its items in the middle column, and shows the chosen item in the reading column.\r\n\r\n**Flag** — a come-back-to-this marker the student raises *during* a question, from a drill or a blind review. It records intent to return, and says nothing about whether the question was answered correctly. Stored in `flagged_questions`.\r\n\r\n**Flagged question** — a question carrying a flag. The Flagged section is the student\'s unfinished business: questions they chose to set aside. Clearing a flag is the student\'s own act (Unflag), not something the system does when they get the question right.\r\n\r\n**Wrong answer** — a question the student actually missed, recorded in `wrong_answer_journal` and worked through in Wrong answers at `/journal`. Distinct from a flag in both meaning and storage: a flag is chosen, a wrong answer is earned. A question can be both, one, or neither. Do not treat the two lists as views of the same thing.\r\n\r\n**Journal entry** — the review of one wrong answer at `/journal/:qid`. The URL is the selection: it resolves only by `qid`, never by the journal row id, and `/journal` does not silently select the first entry. Its review fields autosave without a visible save state.\r\n\r\n**Ask** — sending a question to the coach from the surface the student is looking at, with their own comment attached. Always labelled "Ask", never with the coach\'s name; the name belongs in messaging itself, not in the furniture that leads to it.\r\n\r\n## Boundaries\r\n\r\nThe Classroom owns presentation and the student\'s own annotations of their work. It does not own the work: assignments, drills, attempts and the question bank all belong to their own contexts and are read here, not authored here.\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# Activity Centre\r\n\r\nThe home for bespoke, coach-led skill exercises (e.g. Negation Station) that a student is assigned and works through conversationally. Distinct from the question-bank MCQ **Drill** engine: Activities are authored content with their own answer keys, not timed runs over `qid` questions.\r\n\r\n## Language\r\n\r\n**Activity Centre**:\r\nThe module that hosts and lists Activities. The student\'s home for assigned skill work that isn\'t question-bank drilling.\r\n_Avoid_: Activities Centre, Skills Centre, Drill Centre\r\n\r\n**Activity**:\r\nOne bespoke skill exercise — a titled, self-contained set of authored items with an answer key, worked through with a coach (e.g. "Negation Station"). The unit a teacher assigns.\r\n_Avoid_: Skill drill, drill (reserved for the MCQ engine), station, module, game\r\n\r\n**Homepage**:\r\nJoshua-facing shorthand for an individual Activity\'s intro/start page: the page that shows the Activity title, item count, overview copy, example, and Start action before the student begins the first Question Set.\r\n_Avoid_: LSAT U landing page, Foyer, Activity Centre list\r\n\r\n**Question Set**:\r\nCodex-facing term for one complete per-question unit inside an Activity: the Question Type, the prompt, the response area, the coach feedback, the credited answer, and the answer key material used to judge the response. In the UI, this should still be labeled "Question N"; when Joshua says "question set," he means this full unit, not the collection of all questions.\r\n_Avoid_: Question collection, drill set, item bank\r\n\r\n**Question Type**:\r\nThe classification pill inside a Question Set that names what kind of statement or reasoning form the student is working with (e.g. "Universal Statement," "Conditional Statement," "Causal Statement"). This is the canonical term for the label currently shown above the prompt.\r\n_Avoid_: Category, tag, badge, label\r\n\r\n**Drill**:\r\nReserved term. The existing question-bank engine: a timed run over multiple-choice questions pulled by `qid`. An Activity is NOT a Drill.\r\n\r\n**Item**:\r\nOne prompt within an Activity that the student responds to (e.g. a single statement to negate). Ordered easy→hard as authored.\r\n_Avoid_: Question (overloaded with the qid question bank), problem, task\r\n\r\n**Credited answer**:\r\nThe author-supplied correct response to an Item, used as ground truth when the coach judges a student\'s response. Some Items carry several acceptable phrasings.\r\n_Avoid_: Solution, key answer, right answer\r\n\r\n**Coach**:\r\nThe conversational AI tutor that presents Items one at a time, reads the student\'s free-text response, and gives immediate supportive feedback against the credited answer. Built on the existing AI-gateway tutor pattern (`analyze-stem-response`).\r\nConditional Rephrase is the exception: its constrained `If ..., then ...` responses are judged by an authoritative local parser, so the Activity works without AI credits or a network request.\r\n_Avoid_: Tutor (reserved for the standalone Tutor surface), bot, grader\r\n\r\n**Verdict**:\r\nThe coach\'s judgement of a single student response: `correct`, `partial`, or `incorrect`. `partial` captures a near-miss the Activity exists to teach (e.g. a polar-opposite instead of a true negation). Drives the assignment score and future weakness analytics.\r\n_Avoid_: Grade, mark, result\r\n\r\n**Activity assignment**:\r\nA per-student issued copy of an Activity, snapshotting its content at assign time and tracking the student\'s progress/score. Mirrors the homework `assignment` pattern; immutable snapshot once issued.\r\n_Avoid_: Homework (that\'s the MCQ-set pattern), task\r\n\r\n### Read, Recall, Colour\r\n\r\nThe four-step vocabulary of the RRC runner, plus the language its grading uses.\r\nThe source method\'s own words for these steps are listed under `_Avoid_` and are\r\nnever used in the product.\r\n\r\n**Chunk**:\r\nAn authored cut of a stimulus, roughly a sentence, carrying the Details that fall inside it. A guide for her eye and the unit content is written in, never a boundary she is held to: she reads, recalls and colours in whatever units she likes.\r\n_Avoid_: Line, segment, piece, part\r\n\r\n**Read** (step 1):\r\nThe silent reading of a chunk. Nothing is spoken.\r\n_Avoid_: Skim, study\r\n\r\n**Retelling** (step 2):\r\nSaying a chunk back out loud in her own words. **Retelling** is the word we use to each other and in code; the label a student reads is always **Tell it back**.\r\n_Avoid_: Reading Recall (its former name, which put "Recall" on two different steps), Say it back (the old student label), translate, translation, paraphrase, summary, repeat-back\r\n\r\n**Colouring In** (step 3):\r\nHer spoken reaction to the chunk she just recalled — a thought, doubt, example, or joke that fits that chunk and no other.\r\n_Avoid_: Quip, comment, annotation, reaction\r\n\r\n**The Recall** (step 4):\r\nThe end of a stimulus, worked with the text gone: she says the whole argument back from memory. Graded on Coverage.\r\n_Avoid_: Retelling (that is step 2), CLIR, recap, rebound, takeaway, The Read (see below: that is now the other number, not this step\'s former name)\r\n\r\n**The Read**:\r\nThe analytical takeaway, and the second graded number: what the argument is DOING, as opposed to what it says. Authored per stimulus as `the_read`, judged as two flags, `read_given` and `read_matches` ([ADR 0025](../../../docs/adr/0025-the-read-is-the-second-number.md)). It is a dimension of the grading, never a fifth step she is asked to perform out loud.\r\n\r\nIt was step 4\'s name until [ADR 0012](../../../docs/adr/0012-the-recall-is-a-recap-from-memory.md) renamed that step to The Recall, and this entry did not exist for the six days between 0012 and 0025, so "The Read" read as simply retired. It is not. On the Report Card, **Your Read** is correct and must not be collapsed into The Recall: they are the reasoning half and the memory half, and they fail differently.\r\n_Avoid_: Takeaway, the point, the gist, analysis\r\n\r\n**the move**:\r\nWhat a stimulus\'s Read turns on, in the judge\'s and the card\'s shorthand: the loophole, inference or conflict the argument runs on. Always defined on a surface before it is used, because "you found the move" means nothing to a student reading it for the first time.\r\n_Avoid_: The trick, the gotcha, the key (reserved for the authored answer)\r\n\r\n**Detail**:\r\nOne of the small authored facts a stimulus contains. PT102.S3.Q1 holds 21.\r\n_Avoid_: Point, fact, bullet, item (reserved for an Activity\'s prompt)\r\n\r\n**Coverage**:\r\nHow many of a stimulus\'s Details she carried, counted across everything she said about it rather than against any one Chunk.\r\n_Avoid_: Accuracy, completeness, recall rate\r\n\r\n**Reciting**:\r\nSaying a stimulus back close to word-for-word. A failure of the Retelling, not a step of its own: Coverage looks perfect and nothing was understood.\r\n_Avoid_: Parroting, copying, verbatim recall, memorising\r\n\r\n**Listing**:\r\nDelivering a stimulus as a flat run of facts rather than a story in her own words. The opposite failure to Reciting: Coverage looks perfect and the argument was never held together.\r\n_Avoid_: Shopping list, itemising, enumerating\r\n\r\n**Report Card**:\r\nWhat a finished section produces: every stimulus graded on its own merits, showing what she missed and what she got perfect, with her own recording playable beside it. One player per stimulus, never per question, because two questions can share a stimulus and she recorded it once.\r\n_Avoid_: Scorecard (informally taken by the MCQ Drill results screen), Verdict (that is the coach judging live, which this surface never does), results, grade, review\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'import { Plus } from \'lucide-react\';\r\nimport HubAssignments from \'@/components/hub/HubAssignments\';\r\nimport { BootcampLessonProgress } from \'@/components/teacher/bootcamps/BootcampLessonProgress\';\r\nimport { StudentIndicatorLedger } from \'@/features/activity-centre/components/StudentIndicatorLedger\';\r\nimport { useDossier } from \'./StudentDossier\';\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Work = every assignment kind for this student in one place.\r\n *\r\n * This reuses `HubAssignments`, which already queries all three real sources\r\n * (`homework_assignments`, `ta_assignments`, `classroom_items`) and carries\r\n * the unassign/delete flows. It groups by kind rather than interleaving one\r\n * newest-first list; each group is already newest-first. Rebuilding it as a\r\n * single interleaved timeline would have meant re-deriving those queries and\r\n * dropping the delete affordances, so the grouping stands until there is a\r\n * reason to trade that away.\r\n */\r\nexport default function DossierWork() {\r\n  const { studentId, openAssign } = useDossier();\r\n\r\n  return (\r\n    <div className="space-y-4 p-5">\r\n      <div className="flex justify-end">\r\n        <button\r\n          type="button"\r\n          onClick={openAssign}\r\n          className="inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 bg-teacher-accent px-3 py-2 text-xs font-semibold text-white transition hover:bg-teacher-accent-hover"\r\n        >\r\n          <Plus className="h-3.5 w-3.5" strokeWidth={2} />\r\n          Assign work\r\n        </button>\r\n      </div>\r\n\r\n      <HubAssignments studentId={studentId} />\r\n\r\n      {/* Renders nothing when there is no stored progress, so it never adds an\r\n          empty block. */}\r\n      <BootcampLessonProgress studentClassId={studentId} />\r\n\r\n      {/* What their Activity work says to teach next, read from the attempts\r\n          they have already made. Also renders nothing when there are none. */}\r\n      <StudentIndicatorLedger studentClassId={studentId} />\r\n    </div>\r\n  );\r\n}\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': "import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';\r\nimport { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';\r\nimport { useAuth } from '@/contexts/AuthContext';\r\n\r\nexport type ClassroomItemType =\r\n  | 'drill'\r\n  | 'homework'\r\n  | 'activity'\r\n  | 'bootcamp_module'\r\n  | 'note'\r\n  | 'session_recap'\r\n  | 'announcement'\r\n  | 'upload_summary'\r\n  | 'weekly_plan_task'\r\n  | 'smartboard_recap';\r\n\r\nexport type ClassroomItemStatus =\r\n  | 'draft'\r\n  | 'assigned'\r\n  | 'in_progress'\r\n  | 'completed'\r\n  | 'archived';\r\n\r\nexport interface ClassroomItem {\r\n  id: string;\r\n  student_id: string;\r\n  class_id: string | null;\r\n  created_by: string;\r\n  type: ClassroomItemType;\r\n  title: string;\r\n  description: string | null;\r\n  content_html: string | null;\r\n  content_markdown: string | null;\r\n  source_table: string | null;\r\n  source_id: string | null;\r\n  status: ClassroomItemStatus;\r\n  unlock_at: string | null;\r\n  due_at: string | null;\r\n  completed_at: string | null;\r\n  priority: number;\r\n  visible_to_student: boolean;\r\n  metadata_json: Record<string, unknown>;\r\n  created_at: string;\r\n  updated_at: string;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Student-side: returns visible, unlocked classroom_items for the signed-in\r\n * user. Keep the explicit student_id filter even though RLS also protects\r\n * student accounts: admins can read every row, and may still open student-side\r\n * routes while testing. Without this scope, an admin sees every student's work\r\n * combined into one calendar.\r\n */\r\nexport function useClassroomItems() {\r\n  const { user } = useAuth();\r\n  return useQuery<ClassroomItem[]>({\r\n    queryKey: ['classroom-items', user?.id],\r\n    enabled: !!user,\r\n    staleTime: 60_000,\r\n    refetchOnWindowFocus: false,\r\n    queryFn: async () => {\r\n      const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n        .from('classroom_items')\r\n        .select('*')\r\n        .eq('student_id', user!.id)\r\n        .order('priority', { ascending: false })\r\n        .order('due_at', { ascending: true, nullsFirst: false })\r\n        .order('created_at', { ascending: false });\r\n      if (error) throw error;\r\n      return (data ?? []) as ClassroomItem[];\r\n    },\r\n  });\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Admin-side: full read of a single student's items (regardless of\r\n * visibility / unlock). Admin RLS bypass.\r\n */\r\nexport function useStudentClassroomItems(studentId: string | null) {\r\n  return useQuery<ClassroomItem[]>({\r\n    queryKey: ['classroom-items-admin', studentId],\r\n    enabled: !!studentId,\r\n    queryFn: async () => {\r\n      const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n        .from('classroom_items')\r\n        .select('*')\r\n        .eq('student_id', studentId)\r\n        .order('created_at', { ascending: false });\r\n      if (error) throw error;\r\n      return (data ?? []) as ClassroomItem[];\r\n    },\r\n  });\r\n}\r\n"}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'import DOMPurify from "dompurify";\r\nimport type { ClassroomItem } from "@/hooks/useClassroomItems";\r\nimport { isReadInMetadata } from "@/lib/markNoteRead";\r\nimport type {\r\n  StudentAssignment,\r\n  StudentTAAssignment,\r\n} from "@/hooks/useStudentAssignments";\r\n\r\nexport const DAY_ZERO_WELCOME_SOURCE_TABLE = "profiles";\r\nexport const DAY_ZERO_WELCOME_SOURCE_ID = "day-0-welcome";\r\n\r\nexport const NOTE_TYPES = new Set<ClassroomItem["type"]>([\r\n  "note",\r\n  "session_recap",\r\n  "announcement",\r\n  "smartboard_recap",\r\n]);\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Classroom notes and assignments are authored ONLY by the teacher/admin, never\r\n * by students. Nothing a student types is ever rendered as HTML anywhere in the\r\n * app (messages and free responses render as React text, which escapes), so this\r\n * sanitizer is not what protects the teacher from students.\r\n *\r\n * <style> is therefore allowed: it lets an authored note carry its own design as\r\n * a self-contained file, which is how the rest of the app already treats teacher\r\n * content (every bootcamp lesson renders its HTML raw).\r\n *\r\n * <script> stays blocked. That is the one that could steal a session, and there\r\n * is no authoring reason to want it.\r\n */\r\nconst NOTE_WRAPPER_ATTR = "data-note-parse-wrapper";\r\n\r\nexport function sanitizeClassroomHtml(html: string) {\r\n  /**\r\n   * The wrapper is a parser fix, not decoration. DOMPurify parses into a whole\r\n   * document and returns only the body, and the HTML parser hoists a <style>\r\n   * that OPENS the document into <head> — so a note whose design block comes\r\n   * first would lose the entire design and render unstyled. Verified in Chrome\r\n   * against DOMPurify 3.4.1: leading <style> is dropped, nested <style> survives\r\n   * with @import, @media, @keyframes and custom properties intact. Parsing\r\n   * inside a wrapper keeps the note in body flow; the wrapper is unwrapped below\r\n   * so the rendered markup is exactly what was authored.\r\n   */\r\n  const sanitized = DOMPurify.sanitize(`<div ${NOTE_WRAPPER_ATTR}="">${html}</div>`, {\r\n    ADD_ATTR: ["loading", "sizes", "srcset", NOTE_WRAPPER_ATTR],\r\n    ADD_TAGS: ["style"],\r\n    FORBID_TAGS: ["script"],\r\n  });\r\n  if (typeof document === "undefined") {\r\n    return sanitized\r\n      .replace(new RegExp(`^<div ${NOTE_WRAPPER_ATTR}="">`), "")\r\n      .replace(/<\\/div>$/, "");\r\n  }\r\n  const template = document.createElement("template");\r\n  template.innerHTML = sanitized;\r\n  template.content\r\n    .querySelectorAll(`[${NOTE_WRAPPER_ATTR}]`)\r\n    .forEach((wrapper) => wrapper.replaceWith(...wrapper.childNodes));\r\n  template.content.querySelectorAll("img").forEach((image) => {\r\n    image.setAttribute("loading", "lazy");\r\n    image.setAttribute("sizes", "(min-width: 720px) 720px");\r\n    if (!image.getAttribute("srcset") && image.getAttribute("src")) {\r\n      image.setAttribute("srcset", `${image.getAttribute("src")} 720w`);\r\n    }\r\n  });\r\n  return template.innerHTML;\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function isDayZeroWelcome(item: ClassroomItem) {\r\n  return (\r\n    item.source_table === DAY_ZERO_WELCOME_SOURCE_TABLE &&\r\n    item.source_id === DAY_ZERO_WELCOME_SOURCE_ID\r\n  );\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function classroomNotes(items: ClassroomItem[]) {\r\n  return items.filter((item) => NOTE_TYPES.has(item.type) && !isDayZeroWelcome(item));\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * A note is unread until it has been opened. `markNoteRead` stamps\r\n * `metadata_json.read_at` on first open.\r\n *\r\n * Read state deliberately does not live in `status`: the database\'s\r\n * classroom_items_status_check rejects the \'started\' value the type declares,\r\n * and the remaining allowed values all mean something about work rather than\r\n * about reading. See src/lib/markNoteRead.ts.\r\n */\r\nexport function isNoteUnread(item: ClassroomItem) {\r\n  return !isReadInMetadata(item);\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function unreadNoteCount(items: ClassroomItem[]) {\r\n  return classroomNotes(items).filter(isNoteUnread).length;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Notes split into unread and read, each newest first.\r\n *\r\n * Unread come first because the reason to open this page is almost always a\r\n * note that just arrived. Within each group the sort is by created_at rather\r\n * than the list\'s incoming order, which is priority-then-due_at and means\r\n * nothing for a note.\r\n */\r\nexport function groupNotes(items: ClassroomItem[]) {\r\n  const notes = [...classroomNotes(items)].sort(\r\n    (a, b) => Date.parse(b.created_at) - Date.parse(a.created_at),\r\n  );\r\n  return {\r\n    unread: notes.filter(isNoteUnread),\r\n    read: notes.filter((note) => !isNoteUnread(note)),\r\n  };\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport type ClassroomWorkRow = {\r\n  key: string;\r\n  sourceTable: string;\r\n  sourceId: string;\r\n  title: string;\r\n  status: string;\r\n  date: string;\r\n  route: string;\r\n  classroomItem?: ClassroomItem;\r\n};\r\n\r\nfunction itemDate(item: ClassroomItem) {\r\n  return item.due_at ?? item.unlock_at ?? item.created_at;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * DEAD as of 2026-08-03 — no runtime caller. `useAssignedTodoFeed` is now the\r\n * single source for "what is assigned to me"; this builder read only\r\n * classroom_items + homework + TA, so it could never see guided drills or\r\n * activity assignments, which is what left the two feeds disjoint. Kept for\r\n * now rather than deleted; remove once nothing references it.\r\n *\r\n * Classroom items are the preferred representation. Source assignments fill\r\n * gaps, keyed by the complete source pair so ids from unrelated tables cannot\r\n * collide.\r\n */\r\nexport function buildWorkRows(\r\n  items: ClassroomItem[],\r\n  homework: StudentAssignment[],\r\n  taAssignments: StudentTAAssignment[],\r\n) {\r\n  const rows = new Map<string, ClassroomWorkRow>();\r\n\r\n  for (const item of items) {\r\n    if (!["drill", "homework", "activity", "bootcamp_module", "weekly_plan_task"].includes(item.type)) {\r\n      continue;\r\n    }\r\n    const sourceTable = item.source_table ?? "classroom_items";\r\n    const sourceId = item.source_id ?? item.id;\r\n    const key = `${sourceTable}:${sourceId}`;\r\n    rows.set(key, {\r\n      key,\r\n      sourceTable,\r\n      sourceId,\r\n      title: item.title,\r\n      status: item.status,\r\n      date: itemDate(item),\r\n      route: `/classroom/item/${encodeURIComponent(item.id)}`,\r\n      classroomItem: item,\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  for (const assignment of homework) {\r\n    const key = `homework_assignments:${assignment.id}`;\r\n    if (rows.has(key)) continue;\r\n    rows.set(key, {\r\n      key,\r\n      sourceTable: "homework_assignments",\r\n      sourceId: assignment.id,\r\n      title: assignment.set_title || "Assigned work",\r\n      status: assignment.status,\r\n      date: assignment.created_at,\r\n      route: `/classroom/${encodeURIComponent(assignment.id)}`,\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  for (const assignment of taAssignments) {\r\n    const key = `ta_assignments:${assignment.id}`;\r\n    if (rows.has(key)) continue;\r\n    rows.set(key, {\r\n      key,\r\n      sourceTable: "ta_assignments",\r\n      sourceId: assignment.id,\r\n      title: assignment.title || "Assigned work",\r\n      status: assignment.status,\r\n      date: assignment.assigned_at,\r\n      route: `/classroom/ta/${encodeURIComponent(assignment.id)}`,\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  return [...rows.values()].sort((a, b) => {\r\n    const aDone = a.status === "completed" ? 1 : 0;\r\n    const bDone = b.status === "completed" ? 1 : 0;\r\n    if (aDone !== bDone) return aDone - bDone;\r\n    return new Date(b.date).getTime() - new Date(a.date).getTime();\r\n  });\r\n}\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'import { useMutation, useQueryClient } from \'@tanstack/react-query\';\r\nimport { supabase } from \'@/integrations/supabase/client\';\r\nimport { useAuth } from \'@/contexts/AuthContext\';\r\nimport type { AssignedTodoRow } from \'@/hooks/useAssignedTodoFeed\';\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Marking assigned work done.\r\n *\r\n * Finishing a bootcamp module (or any assignment whose progress lives outside\r\n * its assignment row) never wrote anything back to the row that put it on the\r\n * student\'s list, so the same item sat on Home for ever. The student can now\r\n * close it out explicitly: the source assignment and its classroom_items mirror\r\n * are both flipped to `completed`.\r\n *\r\n * Nothing is deleted. Progress rows, attempts and bootcamp state are untouched,\r\n * and the item keeps showing — as "completed" — in the classroom\'s Assigned\r\n * work panel. Only the "what to do next" surfaces stop counting it.\r\n */\r\n\r\n/** Source tables that carry their own student-updatable `status` column. */\r\nconst STATUS_SOURCES = new Set([\r\n  \'homework_assignments\',\r\n  \'ta_assignments\',\r\n  \'guided_drill_assignments\',\r\n  \'activity_assignments\',\r\n  \'weekly_plan_items\',\r\n]);\r\n\r\nexport async function completeAssignedWork(\r\n  row: Pick<AssignedTodoRow, \'sourceTable\' | \'refId\'>,\r\n  studentId: string,\r\n): Promise<void> {\r\n  const nowIso = new Date().toISOString();\r\n\r\n  if (STATUS_SOURCES.has(row.sourceTable)) {\r\n    const { error } = await supabase\r\n      .from(row.sourceTable as \'homework_assignments\')\r\n      .update({ status: \'completed\' })\r\n      .eq(\'id\', row.refId)\r\n      .eq(\'student_id\', studentId);\r\n    // A source row the student cannot update (or that no longer exists) must\r\n    // not block the classroom_items mirror below, which is what the lists read.\r\n    if (error) console.warn(\'[completeAssignedWork] source update failed\', error.message);\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  const mirror = supabase\r\n    .from(\'classroom_items\')\r\n    .update({ status: \'completed\', completed_at: nowIso })\r\n    .eq(\'student_id\', studentId)\r\n    .neq(\'status\', \'completed\');\r\n\r\n  const { error: mirrorError } =\r\n    row.sourceTable === \'classroom_items\'\r\n      ? await mirror.eq(\'id\', row.refId)\r\n      : await mirror.eq(\'source_table\', row.sourceTable).eq(\'source_id\', row.refId);\r\n\r\n  if (mirrorError) throw mirrorError;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/** React-query wrapper: completes the row, then refreshes every assigned feed. */\r\nexport function useCompleteAssignedWork() {\r\n  const { user } = useAuth();\r\n  const queryClient = useQueryClient();\r\n\r\n  return useMutation({\r\n    mutationFn: async (row: Pick<AssignedTodoRow, \'sourceTable\' | \'refId\'>) => {\r\n      if (!user) throw new Error(\'not signed in\');\r\n      await completeAssignedWork(row, user.id);\r\n    },\r\n    onSuccess: () => {\r\n      for (const key of [\r\n        [\'classroom-items\'],\r\n        [\'todo-feed:guided\'],\r\n        [\'todo-feed:activity\'],\r\n        [\'guided-drill-assignments\'],\r\n        [\'student-assignments\'],\r\n        [\'student-ta-assignments\'],\r\n        [\'weekly-plan\'],\r\n      ]) {\r\n        void queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: key });\r\n      }\r\n    },\r\n  });\r\n}\r\n'}]

Without this, teachers have no visibility\r\n  11:  * into how far the student has gotten — only localStorage knew.\r\n  12:  *\r\n  13:  * Behavior:\r\n  14:  *   - On first call with completedDays > 0, flips status to 'in_progress'.\r\n  15:  *   - Debounces writes (~1s) to avoid hammering the DB.\r\n  16:  *   - When completedDays.length >= totalDays, sets status='completed'\r\n  17:  *     and completed_at = now().\r\n  18:  */\r\n  19: export function useBootcampProgress(opts: {\r\n  20:   bootcampId: string;\r\n  21:   completedDays: number[];\r\n  22:   totalDays: number;\r\n  23: }) {\r\n  24:   const { bootcampId, completedDays, totalDays } = opts;\r\n  25:   const { user } = useAuth();\r\n  26:   const lastWriteRef = useRef<string>('');\r\n  27: \r\n  28:   // Find the active classroom_items row for this student + bootcamp\r\n  29:   const { data: itemId } = useQuery<string | null>({\r\n  30:     queryKey: ['bootcamp-item', user?.id, bootcampId],\r\n  31:     enabled: !!user && !!bootcampId,\r\n  32:     queryFn: async () => {\r\n  33:       const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n  34:         .from('classroom_items')\r\n  35:         .select('id, source_id, metadata_json, status')\r\n  36:         .eq('student_id', user!.id)\r\n  37:         .eq('type', 'bootcamp_module')\r\n  38:         .neq('status', 'archived')\r\n  39:         .order('created_at', { ascending: false });\r\n  40:       if (error) throw error;\r\n  41:       const match = (data ?? []).find((r) =>\r\n  42:         classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(\r\n  43:           {\r\n  44:             source_id: r.source_id,\r\n  45:             metadata_json: r.metadata_json as Record<string, unknown> | null,\r\n  46:           },\r\n  47:           bootcampId,\r\n  48:         ),\r\n  49:       );\r\n  50:       return match?.id ?? null;\r\n  51:     },\r\n  52:     staleTime: 60_000,\r\n  53:   });\r\n  54: \r\n  55:   useEffect(() => {\r\n  56:     if (!itemId || totalDays <= 0) return;\r\n  57:     const sorted = [...new Set(completedDays)].sort((a, b) => a - b);\r\n  58:     const percent = Math.round((sorted.length / totalDays) * 100);\r\n  59:     const allDone = sorted.length >= totalDays;\r\n  60:     const signature = `${sorted.join(',')}|${allDone}`;\r\n  61:     if (signature === lastWriteRef.current) return;\r\n  62: \r\n  63:     const t = setTimeout(async () => {\r\n  64:       try {\r\n  65:         const patch: Parameters<typeof updateClassroomItem>[1] = {\r\n  66:           metadata_json: {\r\n  67:             bootcamp_id: bootcampId,\r\n  68:             progress: {\r\n  69:               completedDays: sorted,\r\n  70:               totalDays,\r\n  71:               percent,\r\n  72:               lastDayAt: new Date().toISOString(),\r\n  73:             },\r\n  74:           },\r\n  75:         };\r\n  76:         if (allDone) {\r\n  77:           patch.status = 'completed';\r\n  78:           patch.completed_at = new Date().toISOString();\r\n  79:         } else if (sorted.length > 0) {\r\n  80:           patch.status = 'in_progress';\r\n  81:         }\r\n  82:         await updateClassroomItem(itemId, patch);\r\n  83:         lastWriteRef.current = signature;\r\n  84:       } catch (e) {\r\n  85:         console.warn('[useBootcampProgress] save failed', e);\r\n  86:       }\r\n"}]

useDossier();\r\n  const { user } = useAuth();\r\n  const [showRecap, setShowRecap] = useState(false);\r\n\r\n  return (\r\n    <div className="space-y-5 p-5">\r\n      <div className="flex justify-end">\r\n        <button\r\n          type="button"\r\n          onClick={() => setShowRecap(true)}\r\n          className="inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 border border-black/15 px-3 py-2 </section>\r\n\r\n      {showRecap && user && (\r\n        <RecapComposer\r\n          studentId={studentId}\r\n          adminUserId={user.id}\r\n          onClose={() => setShowRecap(false)}\r\n ## assistant

The current student feed already merges several assignment sources, but the teacher dossier uses a narrower set. The main product decisions are what counts as completed, whether repeated assignments remain in history, when “in progress” begins, and which reading records belong in My Work. I sent the source evidence and recommendations to the main task. No files were edited.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'import { useQuery, useQueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";\r\nimport { useClassId } from "@/hooks/useClassId";\r\nimport {\r\n  getWrongAnswerSmartRows,\r\n  type WrongAnswerSmartRow,\r\n} from "@/lib/journalService";\r\n\r\nexport function journalEntriesQueryKey(classId: string) {\r\n  return ["journal", "entries", classId] as const;\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function useJournalEntries(enabled = true) {\r\n  const { classId, loading: classLoading } = useClassId();\r\n  const query = useQuery<WrongAnswerSmartRow[]>({\r\n    queryKey: journalEntriesQueryKey(classId),\r\n    enabled: enabled && !!classId,\r\n    staleTime: 60_000,\r\n    refetchOnWindowFocus: false,\r\n    queryFn: () => getWrongAnswerSmartRows(classId),\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  return {\r\n    ...query,\r\n    classId,\r\n    isLoading: classLoading || query.isLoading,\r\n  };\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function useInvalidateJournalEntries() {\r\n  const { classId } = useClassId();\r\n  const queryClient = useQueryClient();\r\n  return () =>\r\n    queryClient.invalidateQueries({\r\n      queryKey: journalEntriesQueryKey(classId),\r\n    });\r\n}\r\n/**\r\n * flaggedService — the service layer `flagged_questions` never had.\r\n *\r\n * A flag is a come-back-to-this marker the student raises mid-question, from\r\n * Drill or either Blind Review flow. It is NOT a record of a missed question;\r\n * that is the Wrong Answer Journal (`wrong_answer_journal`), a different table\r\n * with a different meaning.\r\n *\r\n * Before this, the table was queried inline in five components. Anything new\r\n * should come through here.\r\n */\r\nimport { supabase } from \'@/integrations/supabase/client\';\r\nimport type { Database } from \'@/integrations/supabase/types\';\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Derived from the generated schema rather than hand-written. The old page\r\n * declared its own local interface listing only six of the eleven columns,\r\n * which is how `class_id`, `level` and `qtype` went missing.\r\n */\r\nexport type FlaggedQuestionRow =\r\n  Database[\'public\'][\'Tables\'][\'flagged_questions\'][\'Row\'];\r\n\r\n/** The shape needed to put a flag back after an accidental unflag. */\r\nexport type FlagRestorePayload =\r\n  Database[\'public\'][\'Tables\'][\'flagged_questions\'][\'Insert\'];\r\n\r\nexport function flagLabel(row: Pick<FlaggedQuestionRow, \'pt\' | \'section\' | \'qnum\'>) {\r\n  return `PT${row.pt}-S${row.section}-Q${row.qnum}`;\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport async function listFlagged(userId: string): Promise<FlaggedQuestionRow[]> {\r\n  const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n    .from(\'flagged_questions\')\r\n    .select(\'*\')\r\n    .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\r\n    .order(\'flagged_at\', { ascending: false });\r\n  if (error) throw error;\r\n  return (data ?? []) as FlaggedQuestionRow[];\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport async function updateFlagNote(id: string, note: string): Promise<void> {\r\n  // `.select()` matters: an RLS-blocked UPDATE returns success with zero rows\r\n  // rather than an error, so without this the UI would report "saved" for a\r\n  // write that never happened.\r\n  const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n    .from(\'flagged_questions\')\r\n    .update({ note: note.trim() ? note : null })\r\n    .eq(\'id\', id)\r\n    .select(\'id\');\r\n  if (error) throw error;\r\n  if (!data || data.length === 0) {\r\n    throw new Error(\'That note was not saved. You may not have permission to edit this flag.\');\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport async function unflag(id: string): Promise<void> {\r\n  const { error } = await supabase.from(\'flagged_questions\').delete().eq(\'id\', id);\r\n  if (error) throw error;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Puts an unflagged question back. The row gets a fresh id (the original is\r\n * gone) but `flagged_at` is preserved, so undo does not silently reorder the\r\n * list under the student.\r\n */\r\nexport async function restoreFlag(\r\n  payload: FlagRestorePayload,\r\n): Promise<FlaggedQuestionRow> {\r\n  const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n    .from(\'flagged_questions\')\r\n    .insert(payload)\r\n    .select()\r\n    .single();\r\n  if (error) throw error;\r\n  return data as FlaggedQuestionRow;\r\n}\r\n/**\r\n * wajNotesService — user-scoped reflection notes keyed by (class_id, qid).\r\n *\r\n * Matches the natural upsert key in wajService. Table: wrong_answer_journal_notes.\r\n * Errors are never swallowed — callers must surface failures.\r\n */\r\n\r\nimport { supabase } from \'@/integrations/supabase/client\';\r\n\r\nexport type ReviewStatus = \'unreviewed\' | \'in_progress\' | \'mastered\';\r\n\r\n/** Student\'s self-assessment of how they did. Drives the journal\'s outcome buckets. */\r\nexport type SelfRating = \'correct\' | \'unsure\' | \'wrong\';\r\n\r\n/** A single freehand pen stroke drawn over the question (input-space points). */\r\nexport interface AnnotationStroke {\r\n  points: number[]; // flat [x0, y0, x1, y1, ...] in 0..1 normalized coords\r\n  color?: string;\r\n  size?: number;\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface WAJNote {\r\n  id: string;\r\n  user_id: string;\r\n  class_id: string;\r\n  qid: string;\r\n  // Legacy reflection fields (kept for back-compat)\r\n  root_cause: string | null;\r\n  trap_pattern: string | null;\r\n  student_note: string | null;\r\n  next_action: string | null;\r\n  review_status: ReviewStatus;\r\n  reattempt_due_at: string | null; // ISO date string YYYY-MM-DD\r\n  // Journal v2 fields\r\n  self_rating: SelfRating | null;\r\n  date_taken: string | null; // ISO date YYYY-MM-DD\r\n  question_summary: string | null;\r\n  wrong_answer: string | null; // chosen answer letter\r\n  wrong_reasoning: string | null;\r\n  correct_reasoning: string | null;\r\n  lesson: string | null;\r\n  other_notes: string | null;\r\n  annotations: AnnotationStroke[] | null;\r\n  title: string | null;\r\n  created_at: string;\r\n  updated_at: string;\r\n}\r\n\r\nfunction toAnnotations(raw: unknown): AnnotationStroke[] | null {\r\n  if (!Array.isArray(raw)) return null;\r\n  return raw.filter(\r\n    (s): s is AnnotationStroke =>\r\n      !!s && typeof s === \'object\' && Array.isArray((s as AnnotationStroke).points),\r\n  );\r\n}\r\n\r\nfunction toNote(row: Record<string, unknown>): WAJNote {\r\n  return {\r\n    id: row.id as string,\r\n    user_id: row.user_id as string,\r\n    class_id: row.class_id as string,\r\n    qid: row.qid as string,\r\n    root_cause: (row.root_cause as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    trap_pattern: (row.trap_pattern as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    student_note: (row.student_note as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    next_action: (row.next_action as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    review_status: (row.review_status as ReviewStatus) ?? \'unreviewed\',\r\n    reattempt_due_at: (row.reattempt_due_at as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    self_rating: (row.self_rating as SelfRating | null) ?? null,\r\n    date_taken: (row.date_taken as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    question_summary: (row.question_summary as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    wrong_answer: (row.wrong_answer as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    wrong_reasoning: (row.wrong_reasoning as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    correct_reasoning: (row.correct_reasoning as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    lesson: (row.lesson as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    other_notes: (row.other_notes as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    annotations: toAnnotations(row.annotations),\r\n    title: (row.title as string | null) ?? null,\r\n    created_at: row.created_at as string,\r\n    updated_at: row.updated_at as string,\r\n'}]

## assistant

The current Review sources use different identities and status rules. LR wrong answers, LR flags, RC attempts, and Activity responses need a typed reference before they can share one queue. I sent the file evidence and the decisions about eligibility, “reviewed,” requeueing, and coverage to the main task. No files were edited.

The caller decides\r\n 159:  * what to do about that; this never invents a zero, because a zero here reads\r\n 160:  * as "answered everything wrong" on the score report.\r\n 161:  */\r\n 162: export async function readLrLegResult(\r\n 163:   userId: string,\r\n 164:   pt: number,\r\n 165:   section: number,\r\n 166:   startedAtIso: string,\r\n 167: ): Promise<LegResult | null> {\r\n 168:   const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n 169:     .from(\'section_history\')\r\n 170:     .select(\'initial_score, initial_total, total_questions, total_time_ms, time_taken_ms, completed_at\')\r\n 171:     .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\r\n 172:     .eq(\'pt\', pt)\r\n 173:     .eq(\'section\', section)\r\n 174:     .gte(\'completed_at\', startedAtIso)\r\n 175:     .order(\'completed_at\', { ascending: false })\r\n 176:     .limit(1);\r\n 177: \r\n 178:   if (error || !data || data.length === 0) return null;\r\n 179:   const row = data[0];\r\n 180:   return {\r\n 181:     correct: row.initial_score ?? 0,\r\n 182:     answered: row.initial_total ?? row.total_questions ?? 0,\r\n 183:     timeMs: row.total_time_ms ?? row.time_taken_ms ?? 0,\r\n 184:   };\r\n 185: }\r\n 186: \r\n 187: /**\r\n 188:  * Read one finished RC leg out of `rc_drill_sessions` + `rc_attempts`.\r\n 189:  *\r\n 190:  * Matched on the passage set rather than a section number: RC sessions record\r\n 191:  * which passages were sat, not which section they came from.\r\n 192:  */\r\n 193: export async function readRcLegResult(\r\n 194:   userId: string,\r\n 195:   passageIds: number[],\r\n 196:   startedAtIso: string,\r\n 197: ): Promise<LegResult | null> {\r\n 198:   const { data: sessions, error } = await supabase\r\n 199:     .from(\'rc_drill_sessions\')\r\n 200:     .select(\'id, passage_ids, completed_at\')\r\n 201:     .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\r\n 202:     .eq(\'status\', \'completed\')\r\n 203:     .gte(\'completed_at\', startedAtIso)\r\n 204:     .order(\'completed_at\', { ascending: false })\r\n 205:     .limit(5);\r\n 206: \r\n 207:   if (error || !sessions || sessions.length === 0) return null;\r\n 208: \r\n 209:   const wanted = [...passageIds].sort((a, b) => a - b).join(\',\');\r\n 210:   const session = sessions.find(\r\n 211:     (s) => [...(s.passage_ids ?? [])].sort((a, b) => a - b).join(\',\') === wanted,\r\n 212:   );\r\n 213:   if (!session) return null;\r\n 214: \r\n 215:   const { data: attempts } = await supabase\r\n 216:     .from(\'rc_attempts\')\r\n 217:     .select(\'is_correct, time_ms\')\r\n 218:     .eq(\'session_id\', session.id)\r\n 219:     .eq(\'is_first_attempt\', true);\r\n 220: \r\n 221:   if (!attempts) return null;\r\n 222:   return {\r\n 223:     correct: attempts.filter((a) => a.is_correct).length,\r\n 224:     answered: attempts.length,\r\n 225:     timeMs: attempts.reduce((n, a) => n + (a.time_ms ?? 0), 0),\r\n 226:   };\r\n 227: }\r\n 228: \r\n 229: export async function readLegResult(\r\n 230:   userId: string,\r\n 231:   run: PracticeTestRun,\r\n 232:   leg: PracticeTestLeg,\r\n 233: ): Promise<LegResult | null> {\r\n 234:   if (!leg.startedAt) return null;\r\n 235:   return leg.kind === \'lr\'\r\n 236:     ? readLrLegResult(userId, run.pt, leg.section, leg.startedAt)\r\n 237:     : readRcLegResult(userId, leg.passageIds ?? [], leg.startedAt);\r\n 238: }\r\n'}]

## assistant

Admissions has no dedicated permission flag; any signed-in user can open its current route. Full practice test runs have no owner field and save only to browser storage. A save failure is silently ignored, and old runs cannot be reliably attributed to an account. I sent the precise code evidence to the main task.

A route that\r\n   * should be public is not wrapped in this component at all.\r\n   */\r\n  allowSignedIn?: boolean;\r\n  /**\r\n   * When true, only admin users may render the children. Non-admin\r\n   * users, regardless of feature flags, see LockedModule.\r\n   */\r\n  requireAdmin?: boolean;\r\n  children: ReactNode;\r\n}\r\n\r\nconst LOCAL_DEV_HOSTS = new Set(["localhost", "127.0.0.1", "::1"]);\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Opt-in only. A plain `npm run dev` now exercises the real permission path, so\r\n * a gating regression is visible locally instead of surfacing after deploy.\r\n * Set VITE_DEV_BYPASS_GATES=1 in .env.local to walk gated routes without the\r\n * matching flags.\r\n *\r\n * It relaxes *flags*, never authentication — the caller applies it only after\r\n * the signed-in check, so no configuration can make this component render to an\r\n * anonymous visitor.\r\n */\r\nfunction isLocalDevAccessAllowed() {\r\n  return (\r\n    import.meta.env.DEV &&\r\n    import.meta.env.VITE_DEV_BYPASS_GATES === "1" &&\r\n    LOCAL_DEV_HOSTS.has(window.location.hostname)\r\n  );\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport default function ProtectedRoute({ flag, requireAdmin, allowSignedIn, children }: ProtectedRouteProps) {\r\n  const { user, authReady } = useAuth();\r\n  const permissions = useUserPermissions();\r\n  const location = useLocation();\r\n\r\n  // Auth first, and nothing before it. `useUserPermissions` reports\r\n  // `loading: authReady && !!user && isPending`, which is *false* while auth is\r\n  // still resolving — so a check on `!user` alone read "not yet known" as\r\n  // "signed out" and bounced every cold deep link to /auth. Holding here until\r\n  // auth is definitively resolved is what makes an assignment link survive.\r\n  if (!authReady) {\r\n    return (\r\n      <div className="min-h-screen bg-background flex items-center justify-center">\r\n        <div className="w-6 h-6 border-2 border-muted border-t-foreground rounded-full animate-spin" />\r\n      </div>\r\n    );\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  // Authentication is intrinsic to this component: every shape of it requires a\r\n  // user. The check used to run only for `requireAdmin` or flagged routes, so a\r\n  // wrapper with `allowSignedIn` and no flag fell through to the unconditional\r\n  // render at the bottom and served protected content to anonymous visitors.\r\n  if (!user) {\r\n    const returnTo = `${location.pathname}${location.search}${location.hash}`;\r\n    return <Navigate to={authUrlWithReturnTo(returnTo)} replace />;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  // After the auth checks, never before: the dev bypass opens locked features\r\n  // on a local machine, not the front door.\r\n  if (isLocalDevAccessAllowed()) {\r\n    return <>{children}</>;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  // Only a true cold read blocks; cached reads and background refetches keep\r\n  // this false, so gated navigations do not flash.\r\n  if (permissions.loading) {\r\n    return (\r\n      <div className="min-h-screen bg-background flex items-center justify-center">\r\n        <div className="w-6 h-6 border-2 border-muted border-t-foreground rounded-full animate-spin" />\r\n      </div>\r\n    );\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  if (requireAdmin) {\r\n    return permissions.is_admin ? <>{children}</> : <LockedModule />;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  if (allowSignedIn) {\r\n    return <>{children}</>;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  const flags = Array.isArray(flag) ? flag : flag ? [flag] : [];\r\n  if (flags.length > 0) {\r\n    if (permissions.is_admin || flags.some((f) => permissions[f])) {\r\n      return <>{children}</>;\r\n    }\r\n    return <LockedModule />;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  return <>{children}</>;\r\n}\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'import { readFileSync } from \'node:fs\';\r\nimport { resolve } from \'node:path\';\r\nimport { describe, it, expect } from \'vitest\';\r\nimport { RAIL_CATALOG, type RailItemId } from \'./railCatalog\';\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * The rail draws a padlock from an item\'s flags. The route enforces its own.\r\n * When the two disagree the padlock is decorative and the student reaches the\r\n * content by typing the URL, which is exactly what shipped for /bootcamps\r\n * (no guard at all) and for the `allowSignedIn` routes (flags never ran).\r\n *\r\n * These tests read src/App.tsx as text rather than rendering it. Mounting the\r\n * route tree drags in Supabase, auth and every lazy page; the question here is\r\n * only what the route table declares, and the text answers it.\r\n */\r\n\r\nconst APP_SOURCE = readFileSync(resolve(process.cwd(), \'src/App.tsx\'), \'utf8\');\r\n\r\n/** The rail item -> the path its route registers under. */\r\nconst ROUTE_FOR: Record<RailItemId, string> = {\r\n  home: \'/classroom\',\r\n  // Notes/recaps and assigned work are the coursework inside the shell, and\r\n  // they carry their own guard. The shell itself (home) stays signed-in-open so\r\n  // a fresh signup can reach the intake.\r\n  notes: \'recaps\',\r\n  work: \':assignmentId\',\r\n  drill: \'drill\',\r\n  testing: \'/testing\',\r\n  bootcamps: \'/bootcamps\',\r\n  activities: \'/activities\',\r\n  journal: \'/journal\',\r\n  flagged: \'/flagged\',\r\n  analytics: \'/analytics\',\r\n  history: \'/history\',\r\n  admissions: \'admissions\',\r\n  messages: \'/inbox/*\',\r\n  // Schedule owns booking: `/booking` is the same shell with the Book tab\r\n  // open, so there is no separate `booking` rail item to map.\r\n  schedule: \'/schedule\',\r\n};\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * The `element={...}` text for a `<Route path="X">`, up to the next `<Route`.\r\n * Good enough to answer "is there a guard, and on what flags".\r\n */\r\nfunction routeElement(path: string): string {\r\n  const start = APP_SOURCE.indexOf(`path="${path}"`);\r\n  expect(start, `no route registered for path "${path}"`).toBeGreaterThan(-1);\r\n  const rest = APP_SOURCE.slice(start);\r\n  const next = rest.indexOf(\'<Route\', 1);\r\n  return next === -1 ? rest : rest.slice(0, next);\r\n}\r\n\r\ndescribe(\'rail catalog agrees with the route table\', () => {\r\n  it(\'every rail item maps to a route this test knows about\', () => {\r\n    for (const item of RAIL_CATALOG) {\r\n      expect(ROUTE_FOR[item.id], `rail item "${item.id}" has no route`).toBeTruthy();\r\n    }\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  it.each(RAIL_CATALOG.filter((i) => i.flags.length > 0))(\r\n    \'a padlocked item is enforced by its route: $id\',\r\n    (item) => {\r\n      const element = routeElement(ROUTE_FOR[item.id]);\r\n\r\n      // A padlock over an unguarded route is the /bootcamps bug.\r\n      expect(\r\n        element.includes(\'ProtectedRoute\'),\r\n        `rail padlocks "${item.id}" on ${item.flags.join(\'/\')} but its route has no ProtectedRoute`,\r\n      ).toBe(true);\r\n\r\n      // The route has to gate on at least one flag the rail padlocks on,\r\n      // or the two are guarding different things.\r\n      expect(\r\n        item.flags.some((flag) => element.includes(flag)),\r\n        `rail padlocks "${item.id}" on ${item.flags.join(\'/\')}, its route gates on something else`,\r\n      ).toBe(true);\r\n    },\r\n  );\r\n\r\n  it.each(RAIL_CATALOG)(\r\n    \'allowSignedIn matches the route: $id\',\r\n    (item) => {\r\n      const element = routeElement(ROUTE_FOR[item.id]);\r\n      const routeAllowsSignedIn = element.includes(\'allowSignedIn\');\r\n\r\n      expect(\r\n        !!item.allowSignedIn,\r\n        routeAllowsSignedIn\r\n          ? `route for "${item.id}" lets any signed-in user through, so the rail must not padlock it (set allowSignedIn: true)`\r\n          : `rail marks "${item.id}" allowSignedIn but its route does not, so the rail unlocks a door the route keeps shut`,\r\n      ).toBe(routeAllowsSignedIn);\r\n    },\r\n  );\r\n});\r\n 209:   const navClass = (active: boolean) =>\r\n 210:     `classroom-nav${active ? " classroom-nav-active" : ""}`;\r\n 211: \r\n 212:   // One predicate, matching what ProtectedRoute actually enforces. Anything\r\n 213:   // else and the rail draws a padlock the route does not honour (or the\r\n 214:   // reverse), which the student discovers by typing the URL.\r\n 215:   //\r\n 216:   // `loading` is "unknown", never "denied": every flag reads false until the\r\n 217:   // permission fetch lands, so treating that as denial padlocks the whole rail\r\n 218:   // for a beat on every cold load.\r\n 219:   const canAccess = (item: Pick<RailItem, "flags" | "allowSignedIn">) =>\r\n 220:     permissions.loading ||\r\n 221:     permissions.is_admin ||\r\n 222:     item.flags.length === 0 ||\r\n 223:     (item.allowSignedIn && !!user) ||\r\n 224:     item.flags.some((flag) => permissions[flag]);\r\n 225: \r\n 226:   // `onPrefetch` fires on hover and keyboard focus, not on click: the point is\r\n 227:   // to have the route\'s chunk parsed before the click lands, so the student\r\n 228:   // never sees the stage fallback at all.\r\n 229:   const navProps = (\r\n 230:     active: boolean,\r\n 231:     unlocked: boolean,\r\n 232:     onClick: () => void,\r\n 233:     onPrefetch?: () => void,\r\n 234:   ) => ({\r\n 235:     className: `${navClass(active && unlocked)}${unlocked ? "" : " classroom-nav-locked"}`,\r\n 236:     disabled: !unlocked,\r\n 237:     onClick: unlocked ? onClick : undefined,\r\n 238:     onMouseEnter: unlocked ? onPrefetch : undefined,\r\n 239:     onFocus: unlocked ? onPrefetch : undefined,\r\n 240:   });\r\n 241: \r\n 242:   const railGroups = resolveRailGroups(railPrefs);\r\n 243: \r\n 321:         <button\r\n 322:           type="button"\r\n 323:           className="classroom-brand"\r\n 324:           onClick={() => go("/classroom")}\r\n 325:         >\r\n 326:           <i>LS</i>\r\n 327:           <span>LSAT U</span>\r\n 328:         </button>\r\n 329: \r\n 330:         <nav aria-label="Classroom navigation">\r\n 331:           {railGroups.map((group) => (\r\n 332:             <div className="classroom-nav-group" key={group.id}>\r\n 333:               <b>{group.title}</b>\r\n 334:               {group.items.map((item) => {\r\n 335:                 const { active, onClick, onPrefetch } = railBehavior(item);\r\n 336:                 const unlocked = canAccess(item);\r\n 337:                 return (\r\n 338:                   <button\r\n 339:                     key={item.id}\r\n 340:                     type="button"\r\n 341:                     {...navProps(active, unlocked, onClick, onPrefetch)}\r\n 342:                   >\r\n 343:                     {item.label}\r\n 344:                     {item.id === "messages" && unlocked && unreadMessages > 0 ? (\r\n 345:                       <span className="classroom-nav-badge">\r\n 346:                         {unreadMessages > 9 ? "9+" : unreadMessages}\r\n 347:                         <span className="sr-only"> unread messages</span>\r\n 348:                       </span>\r\n 349:                     ) : null}\r\n 350:                     {item.section && unlocked ? <em>›</em> : null}\r\n 351:                     {!unlocked ? (\r\n 352:                       <Lock className="classroom-nav-lock" aria-hidden="true" />\r\n 353:                     ) : null}\r\n 354:                   </button>\r\n 355:                 );\r\n 356:               })}\r\n'}]

## assistant

The smallest lock is an admin guard on `/classroom/admissions` plus matching admin-only rail behavior. That blocks student navigation and direct URLs while preserving Joshua’s access. The school figures are bundled static data, so a UI lock does not make those figures confidential. I sent exact files, lines, and test changes to the main task.
